## Palace of the Moon

The Palace of the Moon was a temple to Selûne located in Caer Corwell on the island of Gwynneth in the Moonshae Isles as of that town's destruction at the end of the 14 century DR.

Location
The Palace was located on the west side of the Court of the Moon, an expansive stone square above the city's port. A balustrade overlooked the water, lined with iron cressets for providing light, and a large staircase lined with statues of old heroes descended to the harbor.

An alabaster statue of Selûne—wearing a veil and with her arms outstretched—sat atop a fountain in the middle of the Court of the Moon in front of the Palace.

Structure
The Palace was a stone and yellow brick building. The front was a large portico characterized by a long colonnade atop a set of steps, behind which were arched stone windows.

History
After Corwell was overrun by the fey of Sarifal following the, the abandoned Palace and Court crumbled, although they remained mostly intact as of the late 15 century DR. One of the arms of the Selûne statue had broken off by that time, and the fountain in which she stood was dry.

When the crew of the Sphinx landed in Caer Corwell in the late 1470s DR seeking the Winterglen Claw, they landed at the Court of the Moon. It was here that they were greeted by High Lady Ordalf, Queen of Sarifal, who pretended to be a representative of the Claw. Her powerful presence caused the illusion of the area falling into twilight and the fountain to begin filling and overflowing. She offered them tainted food at a table in the Palace's portico, but only Lord Aldon Kendrick was fool enough to partake.

## Pasha's Sabban

The Pasha's Sabban was a unique sector within the city of Calimport where the syl-pasha, ruler of Calimshan, lived and worked. Though nestled within the heart of the city's Palace Ward, it was considered its own entity and designed to be entirely self-sufficient from the rest of Calimport. Whilst such a thing was standard for most sabbans, it was especially important for the Pasha's Sabban, given the city's long history of coups and sieges upon the syl-pasha's domain.

Description
The Pasha's Sabban was by far the smallest sabban in all of Calimport. The area was even smaller than some of the city's drudachs. It had once been made up of two distinct drudachs, one where the syl-pasha lived and one where he worked, but over time most of those delineating walls fell or were replaced by other structures.

As far as the vast majority of Calimpanni knew, there was only one entrance into this area - the Caleph's Gate. The several other ways in and out of the area were a closely guarded secret, with only about half of them shared with the syl-pasha's sultans and his few trusted advisors and protectors. Some of the rumored entrances talked about amongst the populace were in fact fabrications, seeded amongst taverns by the syl-pasha's agents. And there were even speculated to be some passages of which the syl-pashas didn't know, having been lost to time with their predecessors, allegedly leading down into such places as the Muzhahajaarnadah or even the Underdark.

The whole sabban was covered by a magical ward, which was instated back during the Sixth Age of Calimshan. In addition to a defensive function, this barrier had the subtle effect of keeping moisture trapped over the course of a day. This typically kept the air within the Pasha's Sabban cooler than in the rest of Calimport. As temperatures dropped during the night, the accumulated moisture would form into small rain clouds atop the ward. By midnight, without fail, the clouds would unleash their moisture in a light shower, replenishing the cisterns of cellars and rooftops within the sabban. Because of this effect, the guards and animals housed within its walls never went without water.

Being that it was part of the Palace Ward, the Pasha's Sabban too was supported by a slightly vaulted, stonework upper street. Braced by both stone and timber supports, it was comprised of solid street avenues and courts. The engineers responsible constructed it in such a manner as to ensure that it would stay elevated even if half of these timber supports were broken. Directly below the palace, there laid an impenetrable mass of stone pilings.

The lower level was often lumped together with those of the Grand and Jewel Wards as the "Upper Muzad", being considered the uppermost layer of the Muzhahajaarnadah. In some areas there were even said to be hidden entrances to the Muzad, including hidden amongst the stone pilings beneath the royal palace.

Defense
The Pasha's Sabban was the most highly defensible position in all of Calimport. 100 members of el Qysaghanni, who directly guarded the syl-pasha and his family, were garrisoned within its walls. In addition to the Imperial Guard, the sabban was guarded by mercenaries, amlakkar who were stationed on the walls, and el Farisan, who guarded the grounds and their buildings.

If needed, the Pasha's Sabban could also receive the aid of the larger contingent of 500 el Qysaghanni troops garrisoned in the abutting Qysaghanni Fortress, whose surrounding defense wall was connected to the walls of the sabban. There were secret tunnels within the walls of both locales, allowing for ease of passage between the two for el Qysaghanni. These secret tunnels connected to practically every building in the Pasha's Sabban. This was necessary so as to ensure that the syl-pasha could be whisked away on a ship in Armada Ward or that sabban troops could be bolstered in the case of an attempted coup.

Whilst in direct service of the syl-pasha, guards from all of these groups were supplied magical weaponry from the royal armory. Generally this amounted to +1 scimitars and +2 spears for the rank-and-file warriors, whilst officers were granted weapons with item enchantments of +2 or greater. Additionally, some officers of el Qysaghanni were outfitted with wands. The armory in which these weapons were stored was no larger than the standard armories of amlakkhans.

By far the greatest defense the Pasha's Sabban had was its magical ward. This prevented entrance into the area through means of inter-dimensional travel and teleportation, though accounted for some exceptions in regards to both, including portals. It also prevented people from entering through means of flight or immaterial phasing, acting as an invisible barrier equivalent to a wall of force. Additionally, those attempting to fly over its walls would be met by a hail of arrows from the stationed guards.

Notable Locations
In 1370 DR, there were a total of 20 buildings that occupied the Pasha's Sabban, the area being comprised of the syl-pasha's -high walled villa estate and support buildings for his slaves, guards, food storage, and more. The vast majority of the buildings in this area were of the highest quality and rose no less than three to seven stories high. Entrance to the villa was guarded by the Pasha's Gate. Within the walls of the Pasha's Villa stood the following structures:
Pasha's Palace The royal home of the syl-pasha. It consisted of a central building, connected to four smaller wings that spread out in a cross shape and were topped with domes of solid gold.
Djenassadh House A building that contained the living quarters of the syl-pasha's family, his wives, and his personal servants.
The Jhasinnadah As the name implied, was the dedicated living quarters of the syl-pasha's jhasinnadah, his royal mixed-gender harem. The jhasinni and jhasinnar lived in separate wings and were kept under close watch.
Minarets of the Bakkals A religious site, consisting of a trio of towers that were used by the bakkals, the ancient priest-kings of Calimshan, to call out prayers over Calimport and wider nation.

The second major area of the sabban was the Houses of Ardor. Similarly structured as a villa, it contained four luxurious palaces that on the surface the syl-pasha entertained guests. Beneath the surface were three-levels of cellars, containing numerous prison cells, some of the highly secretive great treasures of Calimport, and portals to important sites across Calimshan.

Around the sabban's southern wall stood its miscellaneous support buildings. A well for man and beast, a garrison, an amlakkhan, three granaries, three warehouses, and four large stables for labor animals and horses.

History

The area was formerly part of the Temple and Learning Quarter, before Syl-Pasha Ralan el Pesarkhal introduced the system of wards in the, superseding the long-standing system of sabbans and drudachs.

Circa -1150 DR, roughly six years after Calimport suffered a blaze known as the Villaflames, the ruling Drakhon Dynasty under the reign of Kamal el Drakhon usurped control over some of the cleared out areas that had been affected, restructuring them and expanding the boundaries of the Pasha's Sabban.

In the  the Evyrtaan Dynasty would have the long standing Pasha's Palace constructed through slave labor.

In the, a group of conspirators set off magical explosions with the intent of destroying the Pasha's Sabban and the adjoining slave drudachs. The conspirator would ultimately succeed in cutting short the reign of Syl-Pasha Fahd el Daosiin, as well as killing his sons. The resulting blaze would later be dubbed the Flag Fires.

Over the course of a few centuries prior to 1365 DR, the areas that would be delineated as the Grand, Jewel, and Palace wards underwent vertical construction by the Circle of Architects, Engineers, and Builders. This was done with the intention of providing both the upper classes and their servants greater areas of mobility, as well as to keep the latter out of sight.

In the, Ralan el Pesarkhal broke ground on a long-term construction project, a defensive barrier he dubbed the Pesarkhal Wall. By the next year it encompassed the Armada, Palace, and Shackles Wards. Ralan planned for it to eventually enclose the entirety of the Central Wards.
